================================================================================
  ABOUT THIS DOCUMENT
================================================================================
  This is the GOLD-STANDARD CKS learning guide — a single source of truth for
  content, structure, visual language and quality bar. It takes a CKA-certified
  administrator from zero security knowledge → CKS-certified → confident
  practitioner who can secure a real cluster end-to-end, WITHOUT ever needing
  official docs, blogs, books or videos again.

  MISSION — every security control is taught once, in depth, and demonstrated
  on ONE real application (anihpj/jobpost) so knowledge compounds instead of
  resetting each chapter. Each topic carries an "Exam lens" (what's graded) AND
  a "Beyond lens" (real-world hardening), with internals included so you never
  have to leave.

  INTENDED AUDIENCE
  -----------------
    • Primary ... CKA-certified administrators preparing for the CKS exam.
    • Secondary . practitioners wanting one deep, exam-AND-production reference
                  for day-to-day Kubernetes security engineering.

  SCOPE
  -----
    IN  ... 100% of the official CKS exam domains (Cluster Setup, Cluster
            Hardening, System Hardening, Minimize Microservice Vulnerabilities,
            Supply Chain Security, Monitoring/Logging/Runtime Security), PLUS
            production "Beyond" security engineering (incident response,
            GitOps security automation, compliance).
    OUT ... general cluster administration (CKA) and application-developer
            patterns (CKAD) are referenced where useful but are not the focus.

  CONVENTIONS
  -----------
    Difficulty, exam-relevance and content types use a fixed symbol system —
    see SECTION 1 (Symbol & Icon Legend) and SECTION 2 (Visual Component
    Catalog). Reading guidance is in SECTION 0. The running project is
    SECTION 5; the exam audit is SECTION 6; the roadmap is SECTION 7; the
    master TOC closes the front matter.


================================================================================
  SECTION 0 — HOW TO READ THIS GUIDE  (the design philosophy)
================================================================================
    THE 5 PROMISES OF THIS GUIDE
    ────────────────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  1. ZERO-ASSUMPTION       Every term is defined the first time it      │
    │                           appears. No "you should already know X."     │
    │  2. SHOW, DON'T TELL       Never 3 paragraphs in a row without a        │
    │                           diagram, table, card, or code block.         │
    │  3. ONE REAL PROJECT       Every control is demonstrated on anihpj/     │
    │                           jobpost — the SAME app, start to finish.     │
    │  4. EXAM + BEYOND          Each topic has an "Exam lens" (what's        │
    │                           tested) AND a "Beyond lens" (real hardening).│
    │  5. SELF-CONTAINED         Internals included ("Under the Hood") so you │
    │                           never need to leave this document.           │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    THE LEARNER'S LADDER  (how difficulty is signposted)
    ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
       🟢 BEGINNER      Concepts, vocabulary, "what & why"
       🟡 INTERMEDIATE  Hands-on, YAML, kubectl, "how"
       🔴 ADVANCED      Internals, attack chains, incident response
       🎯 EXAM TOPIC    Directly graded on the CKS performance exam
       🔐 SECURITY-CRIT High-weight concept — attacks or is attacked directly
       🧪 LAB           A hands-on, timed, exam-style task

    HOW TO USE THIS GUIDE
    ─────────────────────
      • Each chapter builds on the previous one — read in order the first pass.
      • Every concept includes anihpj/jobpost real examples.
      • 🟢 = Beginner | 🟡 = Intermediate | 🔴 = Advanced.
      • 🎯 = CKS Exam Topic (directly tested) — drill these hardest.
      • Do the 🧪 Practice Questions at the end of every chapter; hands-on >
        reading.
      • Returning practitioner? Jump to a Part by its TOC anchor; each chapter
        is self-contained behind its learning-objectives block.

    CKS vs CKA — WHY THE PATTERN CHANGES
    -------------------------------------
      CKA (the prerequisite) teaches you to BUILD and OPERATE a cluster. CKS
      teaches you to ATTACK your own assumptions about that cluster and CLOSE
      every gap. Where CKA chapters ask "how do I make this work?", CKS
      chapters ask "how would an attacker break this, and what's the exact
      control that stops them?" — that adversarial framing is why almost every
      chapter here opens with a threat and closes with a verified fix.

    PREREQUISITES & YOUR TOOLKIT  (what you need before Chapter 1)
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  🎓 An ACTIVE CKA certification — mandatory, the exam is literally    │
    │     unavailable without it (this guide assumes CKA-level fluency)    │
    │  💻 Strong kubectl/terminal fluency — you will be on a live terminal │
    │  🐧 Linux comfort: systemctl, journalctl, ps, netstat, ss, chmod     │
    │  🔒 TLS/PKI basics: certificates, private keys, CAs, HTTPS           │
    │  💾 A practice cluster: minikube/kind/k3s (2+ nodes for NetPol labs) │
    │  🧰 Tools installed as you go: kube-bench, trivy, falco, cosign,     │
    │     syft, kubesec — each introduced in the chapter that needs it     │
    │  🌐 Internet — to pull images and, on the OPEN-BOOK exam,            │
    │     kubernetes.io/docs (bookmark 15+ pages before exam day)          │
    │  ⏱️ Budget ~50-60 hours; Appendix G has a 10-week plan (7-11h/week). │
    │  🐍 The example app lives in Dem/anihpj (Django, PostgreSQL).        │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

================================================================================
  SECTION 2 — THE VISUAL COMPONENT CATALOG  (the art direction)
================================================================================
    These are the building blocks used to render content. The rule:
    "NEVER a wall of text." Every subsection must contain >=1 of these.
    Each entry = [WHAT it is] + [WHEN to use it].

    2.1  THREAT/CONTROL CARD GRID    — 2-4 cards, icon + title + 1-line idea.
         WHEN: introduce a set of sibling threats or controls (e.g. 5 default
         K8s insecurities).

    2.2  ANATOMY CALLOUT             — label every field of a YAML/command.
         WHEN: first time a security manifest or command is shown.

    2.3  COMPARISON TABLE            — side-by-side feature/tool matrix.
         WHEN: "X vs Y" decisions (AppArmor vs Seccomp, OPA vs Kyverno).

    2.4  DECISION TREE 🧭            — branching "which control do I use?".
         WHEN: choosing among options (which PSS level, which sandbox runtime).

    2.5  ATTACK-CHAIN / FLOW DIAGRAM — numbered stages with arrows, attacker
         action LEFT, CKS defense RIGHT. WHEN: any multi-stage attack or
         request pipeline (API request lifecycle, MITRE phases).

    2.6  ARCHITECTURE / ZONE DIAGRAM — boxed components + trust boundaries +
         legend. WHEN: showing defense-in-depth layers spatially.

    2.7  MENTAL-MODEL / ANALOGY 🧠  — relate a security concept to everyday
         life. WHEN: a concept is abstract (NetworkPolicy = building gates).

    2.8  MUSCLE-MEMORY DRILL 🔑     — grouped, copy-paste commands with notes.
         WHEN: end of every chapter ("commands to remember under pressure").

    2.9  YAML SKELETON (annotated)  — minimal manifest, every security field
         commented. WHEN: teaching the shape of a hardened resource.

    2.10 UNDER THE HOOD 🔧          — deep-dive (kernel syscalls, admission
         internals, etcd encryption flow). WHEN: once or twice per chapter.

    2.11 EXAM GOTCHA BOX ⚠️        — the trap + why it happens + the fix.
         WHEN: a place learners reliably fail (AppArmor vs Seccomp confusion,
         wrong PSS mode).

    2.12 SPEED TIP ⚡              — exam time-saver (aliases, dry-run,
         verification one-liners).

    2.13 SCENARIO BOX 🚨           — "attacker does X" narrative case study
         anchored to anihpj/jobpost. WHEN: motivating why a control exists.

    2.14 INCIDENT-RESPONSE FLOW 🩺 — detect -> contain -> investigate ->
         eradicate -> recover -> learn tree. WHEN: any breach scenario.

    2.15 BEFORE / AFTER SPLIT-PANEL — "insecure default" vs "CKS-hardened"
         two-panel. WHEN: motivating why a control exists (used constantly).

    2.16 SEVERITY / STATUS PILLS    — 🔴🟠🟡🟢 badges joined by arrows.
         WHEN: CVE severity, risk levels, maturity stages.

    2.17 STAT / WEIGHT BARS         — exam-domain %, study-time budget.
         WHEN: quantitative emphasis.

    2.18 RELATIONSHIP / TRUST MAP   — central node with labeled spokes.
         WHEN: "how everything connects" (4Cs layers, tool ecosystem).

    2.19 KEY TAKEAWAYS BANNER       — ✅ bullet recap + 🎯 exam relevance +
         🔑 commands. WHEN: end of every chapter.

    2.20 VISUAL SUMMARY / CHEAT SHEET — one big picture that re-tells the
         whole chapter in a single diagram + card-grid. WHEN: closing every
         chapter. (RULE: every diagram-only block also carries a short prose
         summary — never a diagram without its text recap.)

    2.21 EXAM QUICK-REFERENCE 🎯    — a second Under-the-Hood toggle holding a
         Task Simulator (numbered steps) + Top Failure Modes + Speed Tips.
         WHEN: closing every chapter, right after the visual cheat sheet.

    2.22 PRACTICE QUESTION CARD 🧪  — exam-style question + Answer +
         Explanation (with YAML/diagram). WHEN: 10+ per chapter, end.

    2.23 EXAM-TOPIC BADGE 🎯        — inline tag marking graded competencies
         and their domain weight.

    2.24 KEY TERM / GLOSSARY POP 📖 — bold term + tight definition box.


================================================================================
  SECTION 3 — THE PER-CHAPTER SCAFFOLD  (fixed anatomy of EVERY chapter)
================================================================================
    Every chapter is assembled from these blocks IN THIS ORDER:

      [A] CHAPTER HEADER      Title + difficulty meta (🟢/🟡/🔴, ⏱️ est time,
                              🔐 domain tags) — the META line.
      [B] CHAPTER INTRO       1-paragraph hook framed on anihpj + what this
                              chapter protects against.
      [C] LEARNING OBJECTIVES "🎯 What you'll be able to DO" — action bullets.
      [D] CKS RELEVANCE       Which exact official domain(s) + weight % this
                              chapter maps to.
      [E] EXAM TIP (optional) A framing note on how the exam grades this topic.
      [F] SECTION BLOCKS      The numbered sections (x.1, x.2, ...). Each one:
                                · 📖 key terms · 🧠 analogy where useful
                                · core explanation broken by visuals
                                · annotated YAML / kubectl as relevant
                                · ⚠️ gotcha · ⚡ speed tip where relevant
      [G] VISUAL SUMMARY      A closing big-picture diagram + prose recap,
                              placed inside the chapter's last section.
      [H] UNDER THE HOOD 🔧   One or two internals deep-dives for the chapter.
      [I] PRACTICE QUESTIONS 🧪  ≥10 exam-style Q&A. EACH has: Question,
                              Answer (commands/steps), Explanation (the WHY) +
                              at least one YAML/diagram/table.
      [J] MUSCLE MEMORY 🔑    Drills — commands to run until they're reflex.
      [K] EXAM GOTCHAS ⚠️    The top traps for this chapter, listed.
      [L] CONNECTED TOPICS 🔗 Cross-chapter links showing the concept web.
      [M] VISUAL CHEAT SHEET  Icon card-grid for rapid recall.
      [N] EXAM QUICK-REFERENCE  Task simulator + failure modes + speed tips.
      [O] CHAPTER NAV          Prev/next links to keep the reading order.


================================================================================
  SECTION 4 — THE PER-PART SCAFFOLD  (fixed anatomy of EVERY part)
================================================================================
      [A] PART BANNER         Title, difficulty band, exam-domain weight,
                              chapter range.
      [B] PART INTRO          What this part is, why it exists, what it
                              unlocks — framed on the anihpj security journey.
      [C] CHAPTER TAGS         A quick list of the chapters in this part.
      [D] CHAPTERS            (each following the SECTION 3 scaffold)
      [E] PART TRANSITION      A short bridge into the next part's theme.


================================================================================
  SECTION 5 — THE RUNNING PROJECT: anihpj / jobpost
================================================================================
    Everything in this guide is HARDENED on ONE real application so security
    controls compound instead of resetting each chapter — you don't just learn
    NetworkPolicy syntax, you watch it close a specific hole in anihpj.

    5.1  WHAT IT IS
         📖 anihpj   = the Django PROJECT (the deployable unit / repo root).
         📖 jobpost  = the Django APP inside it (a job-board: post & browse
                       jobs, skills, employers, locations).
         Data: Python / Django, PostgreSQL in Kubernetes (SQLite in local dev).

    5.2  THE DATA MODEL  (what a breach would actually expose)
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  User (django.contrib.auth)                                    │
         │     │ 1─1                                                       │
         │     ▼                                                          │
         │  Author(company, designation)                                  │
         │     │ 1─many (jobpost)                                          │
         │     ▼                                                          │
         │  JobPost(title, description, salary, type, slug, expiry, date) │
         │     ▲ 1─1            ▲ many─many                                │
         │     │                │                                         │
         │  Location(...)     Skills(name)                                │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         Why this matters for CKS: every model here is candidate-facing PII
         (names, employer relationships, application data) — this is exactly
         the kind of data GDPR/PCI-DSS/SOC 2 require you to protect with
         encryption at rest (Ch 19), RBAC (Ch 9), and audit logging (Ch 28).

    5.3  THE SECURITY JOURNEY  (the through-line)
         🔴 Day 0 (insecure default): runs as root, no NetworkPolicy, secrets
            in plaintext base64, dashboard open, no audit logging, no runtime
            detection. This is what a freshly `kubectl apply`'d cluster looks
            like — and it is the starting state referenced in every "BEFORE"
            box throughout this guide.
         🟢 End state (CKS-hardened): non-root UID 1000, all capabilities
            dropped, default-deny NetworkPolicy with explicit allows, Secrets
            encrypted at rest, images scanned + signed + admission-verified,
            Falco + audit logs + incident-response playbooks active. This is
            the "AFTER" state, and Chapter 33 walks the FULL transformation
            step-by-step, reusing every earlier chapter's control.
         📖 Why this progression, and not "secure from chapter 1"? Because the
            exam — and real jobs — hand you an ALREADY-RUNNING insecure
            cluster. Learning to progressively harden a live system (without
            downtime, one layer at a time) is the actual skill being tested.

    5.4  TARGET HARDENED ARCHITECTURE  (what we build by Chapter 33)
         ┌─────────────────────── NAMESPACE: anihpj-prod ───────────────────┐
         │  🌍 Ingress (TLS, cert-manager)     🔍 Trivy-scanned, ✍️ signed   │
         │              │                                                    │
         │       🧯 default-deny NetworkPolicy + explicit allows             │
         │        ┌─────┼───────────────┐                                   │
         │     📦 anihpj-0 📦 anihpj-1 📦 anihpj-2 (non-root, drop ALL caps) │
         │        │  🔒 Secret (encrypted at rest)  ⛓️ Seccomp  🏰 AppArmor  │
         │        ▼                                                          │
         │       🔌 Service: anihpj-db (ClusterIP only)                      │
         │        │                                                         │
         │     🐘 postgres-0  (non-root, readOnlyRootFS, own NetPol)        │
         │                                                                   │
         │  🔑 RBAC (least-priv SAs)  🦅 Falco  📋 Audit logs  🎓 PSS Restricted│
         └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    5.5  WHERE THE PROJECT APPEARS (mapping) — every part hardens anihpj:
         P1 understand the threat model · P2 measure + secure the baseline ·
         P3 lock down API/RBAC/access · P4 harden the OS/kernel · P5 lock down
         every pod · P6 secure the supply chain · P7 detect + respond at
         runtime · P8 do it all end-to-end, live · P9 prove it on exam day.


================================================================================
  SECTION 6 — CKS EXAM AT A GLANCE  &  OFFICIAL COMPETENCY AUDIT
================================================================================
    Everything below is the full exam audit: official domain weights, the
    CNCF-topic-to-chapter map, the CKA/CKS/CKAD ladder, top-10 mistakes,
    exam-day terminal setup, documentation bookmarks, the security tool
    arsenal, the defense-in-depth stack, and the minute-by-minute exam-day
    strategy. Nothing in the official CKS curriculum is left unmapped.

    CKS EXAM DOMAINS & WEIGHTS
    ───────────────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  CKS EXAM BREAKDOWN — OFFICIAL CNCF CURRICULUM (v1.31)              │
    │  2 hours | ~15-20 performance-based tasks | Pass: 67%                │
    │  ⚠️  PREREQUISITE: Must hold an ACTIVE CKA certification             │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                                          │
    │ ██████████████░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░  15%  CLUSTER SETUP                  │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────   │
    │  • Use Network security policies to restrict cluster level access        │
    │  • Use CIS benchmark to review the security configuration of             │
    │    Kubernetes components (etcd, kubelet, kubedns, kubeapi)               │
    │  • Properly set up Ingress with TLS                                      │
    │  • Protect node metadata and endpoints                                   │
    │  • Verify platform binaries before deploying                             │
    │                                                                          │
    │ ██████████████░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░  15%  CLUSTER HARDENING              │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────   │
    │  • Use Role Based Access Controls to minimize exposure                   │
    │  • Exercise caution in using service accounts e.g. disable defaults,     │
    │    minimize permissions on newly created ones                            │
    │  • Restrict access to Kubernetes API                                     │
    │  • Upgrade Kubernetes to avoid vulnerabilities                           │
    │                                                                          │
    │ █████████░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░  10%  SYSTEM HARDENING               │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────   │
    │  • Minimize host OS footprint (reduce attack surface)                    │
    │  • Using least-privilege identity and access management                  │
    │  • Minimize external access to the network                               │
    │  • Appropriately use kernel hardening tools such as AppArmor, seccomp    │
    │                                                                          │
    │ ██████████████████░░░░░░░░░░░░░░  20%  MINIMIZE MICROSERVICE VULNS     │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────   │
    │  • Use appropriate pod security standards                                │
    │  • Manage Kubernetes secrets                                             │
    │  • Understand and implement isolation techniques (multi-tenancy,         │
    │    sandboxed containers, etc.)                                           │
    │  • Implement Pod-to-Pod encryption (Cilium, Istio)                       │
    │                                                                          │
    │ ██████████████████░░░░░░░░░░░░░░  20%  SUPPLY CHAIN SECURITY            │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────   │
    │  • Minimize base image footprint                                         │
    │  • Understand your supply chain (e.g. SBOM, CI/CD, artifact repos)       │
    │  • Secure your supply chain (permitted registries, sign and validate     │
    │    artifacts, etc.)                                                      │
    │  • Perform static analysis of user workloads and container images        │
    │    (e.g. Kubesec, KubeLinter)                                            │
    │                                                                          │
    │ ██████████████████░░░░░░░░░░░░░░  20%  MONITORING, LOGGING & RT SEC    │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────   │
    │  • Perform behavioral analytics to detect malicious activities           │
    │  • Detect threats within physical infrastructure, apps, networks,        │
    │    data, users and workloads                                             │
    │  • Investigate and identify phases of attack and bad actors within        │
    │    the environment                                                       │
    │  • Ensure immutability of containers at runtime                          │
    │  • Use Kubernetes audit logs to monitor access                           │
    │                                                                          │
    │  TOTAL: 100%  |  PASSING SCORE: 67%  |  FORMAT: Hands-on CLI tasks      │
    │  Certification valid for 2 years from date of passing                    │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    [CKS EXAM DOMAIN → CHAPTER MAPPING — Official CNCF topics matched to this guide]
    ┌───────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  CNCF TOPIC                   │  COVERED IN THIS GUIDE                        │
    ├───────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────┤

    ├───────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  CLUSTER HARDENING (15%)      │                                               │
    │  RBAC to minimize exposure    │  Ch 9: RBAC for Security                      │
    │  Caution with ServiceAccounts │  Ch 10: Service Accounts                      │
    │  Restrict API server access   │  Ch 8: Restricting API Server Access          │
    │  Upgrade to avoid vulns       │  Ch 12: Kubernetes Version Management        │
    ├───────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  SYSTEM HARDENING (10%)       │                                               │
    │  Minimize host OS footprint   │  Ch 13: Reducing the OS Attack Surface       │
    │  Least-privilege IAM          │  Ch 16: Open Ports, Packages & IAM Minim.    │
    │  Minimize external net access │  Ch 16: Open Ports, Packages & IAM Minim.    │
    │  Kernel hardening tools       │  Ch 14: AppArmor + Ch 15: Seccomp            │
    ├───────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  MINIMIZE MICROSERVICE VULNS  │                                               │
    │  (20%)                        │                                               │
    │  Pod Security Standards       │  Ch 17: Pod Security Standards (PSS)         │
    │  Manage Kubernetes Secrets    │  Ch 19: Secrets Encryption + Ch 20: Vault    │
    │  Isolation techniques         │  Ch 21: Container Runtime Sandboxing         │
    │  Pod-to-Pod encryption        │  Ch 22: mTLS & Service Mesh Security         │
    ├───────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  SUPPLY CHAIN SECURITY (20%)  │                                               │
    │  Minimize base image footprint│  Ch 23: Image Security & Scanning            │
    │  Understand supply chain      │  Ch 27: SBOM & SLSA                           │
    │  Secure supply chain          │  Ch 24: Admission Controllers + Ch 26: cosign│
    │  Static analysis              │  Ch 25: Static Analysis (Kubesec/KubeLinter) │
    ├───────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  MONITORING, LOGGING & RT     │                                               │

    │  Detect threats               │  Ch 30: Detecting & Responding to Threats    │
    │  Investigate attack phases    │  Ch 30: Detecting & Responding to Threats    │
    │  Immutability of containers   │  Ch 31: Immutable Containers                 │
    │  Audit logs to monitor access │  Ch 28: Kubernetes Audit Logging             │
    └───────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    CKS vs CKA vs CKAD — THE KUBERNETES CERTIFICATION LADDER
    ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                CKS               CKA              CKAD       │
    │  Focus:     Security            Admin            Developer   │
    │                                                              │
    │  You own:   Hardening &         The CLUSTER      The APPS    │
    │             securing the        itself           on the      │
    │             ENTIRE cluster                       cluster     │
    │                                                              │
    │  Topics:    RBAC, AppArmor,     Install,         Deploy,     │
    │             Seccomp, Falco,     upgrade,         configure,  │
    │             supply chain,       backup etcd,     scaling,    │
    │             image scanning,     certificates,    Helm, CI/CD │
    │             network policies,   troubleshoot                 │
    │             OPA, runtime sec.   nodes/cluster                │
    │                                                              │
    │  Think of:  🛡️ Security Guard   🏗️ Building      🎨 Decorat. │
    │             protecting          the house        the rooms   │
    │             the house                                        │
    │                                                              │
    │  Prereq:    Active CKA cert     Linux basics     Linux basics│
    │             required!                                        │
    │                                                              │
    │  THIS GUIDE = CKS (we lock down and secure the house)        │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    CKS EXAM STRATEGY — TOP 10 MISTAKES TO AVOID
    ─────────────────────────────────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  ❌ COMMON MISTAKES                 ✅ WHAT TO DO INSTEAD    │
    │                                                              │
    │  1. Mixing up AppArmor vs         Know: AppArmor = profile  │
    │     Seccomp (most common          on node. Seccomp = syscall │
    │     exam mistake)                 filter in pod spec        │
    │                                                              │
    │  2. Forgetting to enable          Always check namespace:    │
    │     PodSecurity admission         kubectl label ns <name>    │
    │     on the right namespace        pod-security.k8s.io/...   │
    │                                                              │
    │  3. Not switching cluster         ALWAYS verify first:       │
    │     context before starting       kubectl config             │
    │                                   current-context            │
    │                                                              │
    │  4. Creating RBAC but not        Test it:                   │
    │     verifying it works            kubectl auth can-i get pods│
    │                                   --as=<user>               │
    │                                                              │
    │  5. Forgetting to verify         Use: kubectl exec pod --    │
    │     NetworkPolicy actually        cat /proc/net/fib_trie    │
    │     blocks traffic                or ping to verify block    │
    │                                                              │
    │  6. Confusing PodSecurity        Enforce = blocks pods      │
    │     admission modes              Audit = just logs          │
    │     (enforce/audit/warn)         Warn = warns but allows    │
    │                                                              │
    │  7. Skipping audit log           Check apiserver manifest   │
    │     configuration flags          in /etc/kubernetes/manifests│
    │                                                              │
    │  8. Not knowing Falco            Know: falco.yaml,          │
    │     rule syntax                  output formats,            │
    │                                  /etc/falco/falco.yaml      │
    │                                                              │
    │  9. Leaving privileged           Always check SecurityContext│
    │     containers untouched         runAsNonRoot: true         │
    │     when fixing questions         allowPrivilegeEscalation   │
    │                                                              │
    │  10. Not using docs              Bookmarks are allowed!     │
    │      (kubernetes.io allowed       Prep 15+ bookmarks before  │
    │      during exam)                 the exam                  │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    CKS EXAM DAY SETUP — YOUR TERMINAL ALIASES & FIRST STEPS
    ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  Run FIRST when your exam terminal opens:                    │
    │                                                              │
    │  alias k=kubectl                                             │
    │  alias kgp='kubectl get pods -o wide'                        │
    │  alias kgs='kubectl get svc'                                 │
    │  alias kgn='kubectl get nodes'                               │
    │  alias kd='kubectl describe'                                 │
    │  alias kaf='kubectl apply -f'                                │
    │  export do="--dry-run=client -o yaml"                        │
    │  export now="--force --grace-period=0"                       │
    │                                                              │
    │  Security-specific shortcuts:                                │
    │  alias kauth='kubectl auth can-i'                            │
    │  alias kaudit='cat /etc/kubernetes/audit-policy.yaml'        │
    │  alias kfalco='systemctl status falco'                       │
    │                                                              │
    │  Check which cluster you're on:                              │
    │  kubectl config current-context                              │
    │  kubectl config get-contexts                                 │
    │                                                              │
    │  ⏱️  These setup steps save 10-15 minutes during the exam!  │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    CKS SECURITY TOOLS COMPARISON — KNOW YOUR ARSENAL
    ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
    [Every CKS candidate must know what each tool does, when to use it, and where it fits]

    ┌───────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┬───────────────┐
    │  TOOL             │  WHAT IT DOES                    │  CKS DOMAIN                  │  EXAM RELEV.  │
    ├───────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┼───────────────┤
    │  kube-bench       │  Scans cluster against CIS       │  Cluster Setup (10%)         │  ⭐⭐⭐ HIGH   │
    │                   │  Benchmark; produces PASS/FAIL/   │                               │               │
    │                   │  WARN report per control          │                               │               │
    ├───────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┼───────────────┤
    │  Trivy            │  Scans container images for CVEs; │  Supply Chain (20%)          │  ⭐⭐⭐ HIGH   │
    │                   │  also scans filesystems & repos   │  Microservice Vulns (20%)    │               │
    ├───────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┼───────────────┤
    │  Falco            │  Runtime threat detection via     │  Monitoring/Runtime (20%)    │  ⭐⭐⭐ HIGH   │
    │                   │  eBPF; alerts on anomalous        │                               │               │
    │                   │  syscalls, shells, file access    │                               │               │
    ├───────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┼───────────────┤
    │  AppArmor         │  Linux kernel module; restricts   │  System Hardening (15%)      │  ⭐⭐⭐ HIGH   │
    │                   │  programs by path/file permissions│                               │               │
    ├───────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┼───────────────┤
    │  Seccomp          │  Linux kernel feature; filters    │  System Hardening (15%)      │  ⭐⭐⭐ HIGH   │
    │                   │  which syscalls a process can make│  Microservice Vulns (20%)    │               │
    ├───────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┼───────────────┤
    │  OPA/Gatekeeper   │  Policy engine; enforces rules    │  Supply Chain (20%)          │  ⭐⭐ MEDIUM   │
    │                   │  at admission time via Rego       │                               │               │
    ├───────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┼───────────────┤
    │  Kyverno          │  K8s-native policy engine; YAML-  │  Supply Chain (20%)          │  ⭐⭐ MEDIUM   │
    │                   │  based policies (no Rego needed)  │                               │               │
    ├───────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┼───────────────┤
    │  cosign           │  Signs & verifies container images│  Supply Chain (20%)          │  ⭐⭐ MEDIUM   │
    │                   │  using Sigstore (keyless or keys) │                               │               │
    ├───────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┼───────────────┤
    │  kubesec          │  Scores K8s pod YAML for security │  Supply Chain (20%)          │  ⭐ LOW        │
    │                   │  risks (caps, root, net, etc.)    │                               │               │
    ├───────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┼───────────────┤
    │  kube-linter      │  Lints K8s YAML for best practices│  Supply Chain (20%)          │  ⭐ LOW        │
    │                   │  (security + production readiness)│                               │               │
    ├───────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┼───────────────┤
    │  gVisor           │  Sandboxed container runtime;     │  Microservice Vulns (20%)    │  ⭐⭐ MEDIUM   │
    │                   │  intercepts syscalls in userspace │                               │               │
    ├───────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┼───────────────┤
    │  Kata Containers  │  VM-level isolation per container │  Microservice Vulns (20%)    │  ⭐ LOW        │
    ├───────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┼───────────────┤
    │  cert-manager     │  Automates TLS cert issuance &    │  Cluster Setup (10%)         │  ⭐⭐ MEDIUM   │
    │                   │  renewal (ACME, self-signed, etc.)│                               │               │
    ├───────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┼───────────────┤
    │  Vault (HashiCorp)│  Enterprise secret management;    │  Microservice Vulns (20%)    │  ⭐⭐ MEDIUM   │
    │                   │  dynamic secrets, auto-rotation   │                               │               │
    ├───────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┼───────────────┤
    │  syft             │  Generates SBOM from container    │  Supply Chain (20%)          │  ⭐ LOW        │
    │                   │  images (SPDX, CycloneDX formats) │                               │               │
    └───────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────┴───────────────┘

    [TOOL LAYERING — where each tool sits in the security stack]
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                                              │
    │  🛡️  DEFENSE-IN-DEPTH TOOL STACK (from outermost to innermost)              │
    │                                                                              │
    │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
    │  │  PRE-DEPLOYMENT (before anything hits the cluster)                   │    │
    │  │  kubesec → kube-linter → Trivy → cosign → SBOM (syft)              │    │
    │  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
    │                                    │                                          │
    │                                    ▼                                          │
    │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
    │  │  ADMISSION (at the cluster gate)                                     │    │
    │  │  OPA/Gatekeeper → Kyverno → Pod Security Admission → Image Webhook  │    │
    │  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
    │                                    │                                          │
    │                                    ▼                                          │
    │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
    │  │  POD RUNTIME (active while pod runs)                                 │    │
    │  │  SecurityContext → Seccomp → AppArmor → readOnlyRootFS → gVisor     │    │
    │  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
    │                                    │                                          │
    │                                    ▼                                          │
    │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
    │  │  CONTINUOUS MONITORING (ongoing, never stops)                        │    │
    │  │  Falco → Audit Logs → kube-bench CronJob → Trivy Operator           │    │
    │  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
    │                                                                              │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    CKS EXAM DAY — MINUTE-BY-MINUTE STRATEGY (120 Minutes)
    ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    [Your complete timeline for the 2-hour CKS exam]

    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  ⏱️  MINUTE 0-5: SETUP RITUAL (DO NOT SKIP — this saves you later!)         │
    │  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │  ☐  0:00  Read the exam instructions carefully (they vary per exam!)         │
    │  ☐  0:30  Set up ALL aliases (copy-paste from your muscle memory):           │
    │          alias k=kubectl                                                     │
    │          alias kaf='kubectl apply -f'                                        │
    │          alias kd='kubectl describe'                                         │
    │          alias kgp='kubectl get pods -o wide'                                │
    │          alias ka='kubectl auth can-i'                                       │
    │          export do="--dry-run=client -o yaml"                                │
    │          export now="--force --grace-period=0"                               │
    │  ☐  1:00  Check YOUR cluster context:                                        │
    │          kubectl config current-context                                       │
    │          kubectl config get-contexts  (note ALL contexts for switching)       │
    │  ☐  2:00  Open your bookmarks in a separate tab (NOT the exam browser)       │
    │  ☐  3:00  Check node status: kubectl get nodes -o wide                       │
    │  ☐  4:00  Identify cluster version: kubectl version --short                  │
    │  ☐  5:00  Take a deep breath. You're ready.                                  │
    ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  ⏱️  MINUTE 5-10: FIRST PASS — SCAN ALL QUESTIONS                            │
    │  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │  • Open ALL questions in separate terminals/tabs                             │
    │  • SKIM each question: DO NOT solve anything yet                             │
    │  • Categorize each question mentally:                                        │
    │    🟢 EASY:   < 5 min (e.g., create RBAC, label namespace for PSS)          │
    │    🟡 MEDIUM:  5-10 min (e.g., write NetworkPolicy, configure audit)         │
    │    🔴 HARD:    > 10 min (e.g., full Falco rule + deployment, AppArmor)      │
    │  • Write down question numbers on scratch paper with category                │
    │  • NOTE: CKS has ~15-20 questions → average ~6-8 min per question            │
    ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  ⏱️  MINUTE 10-75: SECOND PASS — SOLVE ALL EASY + MEDIUM (~65 min)          │
    │  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │  • Solve 🟢 EASY questions first (build confidence + score fast)             │
    │  • Then solve 🟡 MEDIUM questions                                            │
    │  • FOR EACH QUESTION:                                                        │
    │    a) READ the question twice (miss keywords = lose points)                  │
    │    b) SWITCH to the CORRECT cluster context FIRST                            │
    │    c) SOLVE the task                                                         │
    │    d) VERIFY: run a check command after every solution                       │
    │       - RBAC: kubectl auth can-i --as=user get pods -n <ns>                 │
    │       - NetworkPolicy: kubectl exec pod -- ping <target>                     │
    │       - PSS: kubectl label ns <name> pod-security... --dry-run               │
    │       - Seccomp: kubectl get pod <name> -o jsonpath='{.spec.securityContext}'│
    │    e) FLAG if unsure, move on (don't get stuck!)                             │
    │  • TARGET: Complete 10-12 questions by minute 75                             │
    ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  ⏱️  MINUTE 75-100: THIRD PASS — HARD QUESTIONS + FLAGGED (~25 min)         │
    │  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │  • Tackle 🔴 HARD questions (you've scored points, now maximize)             │
    │  • Revisit flagged questions with fresh eyes                                 │
    │  • USE YOUR BOOKMARKS aggressively — they're allowed!                        │
    │  • For YAML-heavy questions:                                                 │
    │    - Use kubectl explain <resource>.spec to find field names                 │
    │    - Use --dry-run=client -o yaml to generate boilerplate                    │
    │    - Copy from kubernetes.io docs examples and modify                        │
    │  • If a question has MULTIPLE sub-tasks:                                     │
    │    - Do each sub-task and verify individually                                │
    │    - Partial credit exists! Even if you can't do all, do what you can         │
    ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  ⏱️  MINUTE 100-115: FINAL VERIFICATION (~15 min)                            │
    │  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │  • Re-verify ALL answers (check each question's cluster context again!)      │
    │  • Common verification commands per question type:                           │
    │    - RBAC:       kubectl auth can-i --list --as=<user> -n <ns>              │
    │    - NetPol:     kubectl describe netpol <name> -n <ns>                      │
    │    - PSS:        kubectl get ns <name> -o yaml | grep pod-security           │
    │    - Audit:      cat /etc/kubernetes/audit-policy.yaml (check syntax)       │
    │    - AppArmor:   kubectl get pod <name> -o yaml | grep apparmor             │
    │    - Seccomp:    kubectl get pod <name> -o json | jq '.spec.containers[].sec │
    │    - Falco:      cat /etc/falco/falco_rules.local.yaml (check rule syntax)  │
    │    - Secrets:    grep encryption-provider-config /etc/kubernetes/manifests/  │
    │  • Did you apply to the RIGHT namespace? Check!                              │
    │  • Did you switch to the RIGHT context? Double-check!                        │
    │  • Are all YAML files saved in the correct directory/location?               │
    ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  ⏱️  MINUTE 115-120: LAST 5 MINUTES — DESPERATION MODE                       │
    │  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │  • DON'T PANIC. 5 minutes is enough to solve 1 more question.                │
    │  • If any question is still UNANSWERED:                                      │
    │    - GUESS intelligently (partial credit > zero credit)                      │
    │    - Write YAML even if incomplete (syntax might get partial points)         │
    │    - For CLI tasks: at least run the command skeleton                        │
    │  • NEVER leave a question COMPLETELY blank                                   │
    │  • Quick wins in last minutes:                                               │
    │    - kubectl label ns <name> pod-security.kubernetes.io/enforce=restricted   │
    │    - kubectl create role <name> --verb=get,list --resource=pods -n <ns>     │
    │    - kubectl create rolebinding <name> --role=<role> --user=<user> -n <ns>  │
    │  • SAVE all terminal output (copy-paste to notepad if unsure)                │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘


================================================================================
  SECTION 7 — THE LEARNING ROADMAP  (9 parts / 38 chapters, zero -> pro)
================================================================================
    LEARNING PATH OVERVIEW
    ──────────────────────
    [ROADMAP ILLUSTRATION: 9-Part journey from security zero to CKS certified]

    Prerequisites:
      • Active CKA certification (MANDATORY — exam will not be available without it)
      • Strong kubectl / terminal fluency (you WILL be on a live terminal)
      • Comfort with Linux: systemctl, journalctl, ps, netstat, ss, lsof
      • Basic understanding of TLS, PKI, and networking concepts
      • Familiarity with YAML (you write it from memory under time pressure)
      [ICON ROW: CKA badge | Terminal icon | Linux penguin | Shield icon | Clock]

    How to Use This Guide:
      • Each chapter builds on the previous one
      • Every concept is demonstrated with anihpj/jobpost real examples
      • 🟢 = Beginner | 🟡 = Intermediate | 🔴 = Advanced
      • 🎯 = CKS Exam Topic (directly tested on the exam)
      • 🔐 = Security-critical concept (high weight on exam)
      • [INTERACTIVE PROGRESS TRACKER BAR]

    [SECURE DEPLOYMENT PIPELINE — the anihpj journey from code to CKS-hardened pod]
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                                           │
    │  💻 SOURCE CODE         🐳 IMAGE BUILD          ☸️  KUBERNETES           │
    │  ─────────────         ──────────────          ──────────────            │
    │                                                                           │
    │  ┌─────────────┐      ┌─────────────┐         ┌─────────────┐           │
    │  │  Developer  │      │  Dockerfile │         │  anihpj Pod │           │
    │  │  writes code│─────▶│  multi-stage│────────▶│  running in  │           │
    │  │  + YAML     │      │  build      │         │  production │           │
    │  └──────┬──────┘      └──────┬──────┘         └──────┬──────┘           │
    │         │                    │                       │                    │
    │         │                    │                       │                    │
    │    ┌────▼──────────┐   ┌────▼──────────┐      ┌─────▼─────────────┐    │
    │    │ GATE 1:        │   │ GATE 2:       │      │ GATE 3:           │    │
    │    │ STATIC ANALYSIS│   │ IMAGE SCAN    │      │ ADMISSION CONTROL │    │
    │    │                │   │               │      │                    │    │
    │    │ kube-linter    │   │ trivy image   │      │ Kyverno verifyImgs│    │
    │    │ kubesec scan   │   │ hadolint       │      │ PSS Restricted    │    │
    │    │ conftest test  │   │ cosign sign   │      │ OPA policies      │    │
    │    │                │   │               │      │                    │    │
    │    │ ✅/❌ PASS/FAIL│   │ ✅/❌ PASS/FAIL│      │ ✅/❌ ADMIT/REJECT│    │
    │    └────────────────┘   └───────┬───────┘      └────────────────────┘    │
    │                                 │                                         │
    │                                 │                                         │
    │    ┌────────────────────────────┘                                         │
    │    │                                                                       │
    │    ▼                                                          RUNTIME     │
    │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
    │  │  GATE 4: RUNTIME PROTECTION (continuously active)                 │    │
    │  │                                                                   │    │
    │  │  🦅 Falco monitors syscalls → detects shells, file reads          │    │
    │  │  📋 Audit logs record ALL API calls → trace every action          │    │
    │  │  🔍 Trivy Operator scans running images → catches NEW CVEs        │    │
    │  │  🛡️ NetworkPolicy blocks unauth traffic → lateral movement stopped│    │
    │  │  🧊 readOnlyRootFS prevents tampering → container stays clean     │    │
    │  │  ⏰ AlertManager notifies on-call → incident response triggered   │    │
    │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
    │                                                                           │
    │  📊 DASHBOARD: Grafana shows security metrics in real-time                │
    │     CVE Count | Falco Alert Rate | Policy Violations | Audit Events      │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    [PREREQUISITE SKILLS CARD — what you need BEFORE starting this guide]
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  BEFORE YOU BEGIN — CKS READINESS CHECKLIST                             │
    │                                                                           │
    │  ✅ MUST HAVE (non-negotiable)                                            │
    │  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │
    │  │  □ Active CKA certification (exam literally unavailable without it) │ │
    │  │  □ Comfortable with kubectl: get, describe, apply, exec, logs       │ │
    │  │  □ Can write Pod, Deployment, Service YAML from memory              │ │
    │  │  □ Understand RBAC basics: Role, ClusterRole, Binding               │ │
    │  │  □ Linux: systemctl, journalctl, ps, netstat, ss, cat, grep         │ │
    │  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │
    │                                                                           │
    │  📚 SHOULD KNOW (strongly recommended)                                    │
    │  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │
    │  │  □ TLS basics: certificates, private keys, CAs, HTTPS              │ │
    │  │  □ Docker: build, push, run; what a Dockerfile does                 │ │
    │  │  □ Basic networking: TCP/IP, ports, DNS, firewalls                  │ │
    │  │  □ Linux file permissions: chmod, chown, setuid                     │ │
    │  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │
    │                                                                           │
    │  🟡 NICE TO HAVE (we'll teach you the rest)                               │
    │  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │
    │  │  □ Awareness of OWASP Top 10 or general security concepts          │ │
    │  │  □ Experience with CI/CD pipelines (GitHub Actions, GitLab CI)     │ │
    │  │  □ Any exposure to HashiCorp Vault or cloud IAM                     │ │

    DETAILED 10-WEEK CKS STUDY PLAN
    ───────────────────────────────
    [This plan assumes 6-8 hours/week total study time across reading + labs]

    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  WEEK 1: FOUNDATION — The Security Mindset                                    │
    │  ─────────────────────────────────────────                                    │
    │  📖 Reading: Chapters 1-3 (Intro, Threat Landscape, Security Architecture)    │
    │  🖥️  Labs:                                                                   │
    │     • Set up Minikube/Kind cluster with security focus                        │
    │     • Deploy anihpj WITHOUT any security (observe default insecure state)     │
    │     • Run kubectl get pods -o yaml and identify: runs as root? privileged?   │
    │     • Explore the cluster: kubectl cluster-info, kubectl get all --all-ns     │
    │  ✍️  Exercises:                                                               │
    │     • Write down 5 things you would attack if you were a hacker               │
    │     • Map the 4Cs model to your anihpj cluster                                │
    │  📝  Self-Check: Can you explain the 4Cs to someone in 5 minutes?            │
    ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  WEEK 2: CLUSTER SETUP — Measuring & Securing the Baseline                    │
    │  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────                    │
    │  📖 Reading: Chapters 4-7 (CIS Benchmarks, Dashboard, NetworkPolicy, TLS)     │
    │  🖥️  Labs:                                                                   │
    │     • Run kube-bench on your cluster, read the report                        │
    │     • Fix at least 5 FAIL/WARN results from kube-bench                       │
    │     • Write a default-deny NetworkPolicy for anihpj namespace                 │
    │     • Write specific allow rules: anihpj→postgres, ingress→anihpj            │
    │     • Verify policies: kubectl exec + ping to test blocks                     │
    │     • Create TLS certificate for anihpj ingress (self-signed or cert-manager) │
    │  ✍️  Exercises:                                                               │
    │     • Create a NetworkPolicy diagram for anihpj (draw all allow/deny paths)   │
    │  📝  Self-Check: Can you write default-deny + allow rules from memory?       │
    ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  WEEK 3: CLUSTER HARDENING — Locking the API & Access Control                 │
    │  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────                    │
    │  📖 Reading: Chapters 8-10 (API Server, RBAC, ServiceAccounts)                │
    │  🖥️  Labs:                                                                   │
    │     • Configure API server flags: audit-log-path, audit-policy-file           │
    │     • Create a new user with minimal RBAC (only jobpost-read access)          │
    │     • Test: kubectl auth can-i --as=newuser get pods -n anihpj               │
    │     • Create ServiceAccount for anihpj with minimal cluster permissions       │
    │     • Disable automountServiceAccountToken where not needed                   │
    │     • Experiment: SA with cluster-admin → see what it can access              │
    │  ✍️  Exercises:                                                               │
    │     • Create an RBAC matrix: who needs what access to anihpj                  │
    │  📝  Self-Check: Can you create Role+RoleBinding from kubectl imperative?    │
    ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  WEEK 4: CLUSTER HARDENING — Nodes, Versions & Upgrades                       │
    │  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────                    │
    │  📖 Reading: Chapters 11-12 (Node Restriction, Version Management)            │
    │  🖥️  Labs:                                                                   │
    │     • Enable NodeRestriction admission plugin                                │
    │     • Practice kubectl version + kubeadm version                             │
    │     • Simulate: find which components need upgrading for a CVE fix           │
    │     • Set up version skew testing: control plane v1.30, nodes v1.29          │
    │     • Cordon/drain a node for maintenance (security update scenario)         │
    │  ✍️  Exercises:                                                               │
    │     • Create a security patch calendar for your cluster                       │
    │  📝  Self-Check: Can you explain what NodeRestriction protects against?       │
    ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  WEEK 5: SYSTEM HARDENING — The OS & Kernel Level                             │
    │  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────                    │
    │  📖 Reading: Chapters 13-16 (OS Hardening, AppArmor, Seccomp, Ports/Minim.)   │
    │  🖥️  Labs:                                                                   │
    │     • Check AppArmor: aa-status; load a custom profile                       │
    │     • Apply AppArmor profile to anihpj pod (annotation)                       │
    │     • Verify: try to access blocked paths from inside pod                     │
    │     • Create Seccomp profile: default.json + block chmod                     │
    │     • Apply Seccomp to anihpj pod and verify syscall blocking                 │
    │     • Run ss -tlnp on node, identify unnecessary open ports                   │
    │     • Stop/disable unnecessary services via systemctl                          │
    │  ✍️  Exercises:                                                               │
    │     • Write a custom AppArmor profile for anihpj (allow only what's needed)   │
    │  📝  Self-Check: Seccomp vs AppArmor — can you explain when to use each?      │
    ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  WEEK 6: MICROSERVICE VULNERABILITIES — Pod-Level Security                    │
    │  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────                    │
    │  📖 Reading: Chapters 17-22 (PSS, SecurityContext, Secrets, Vault, Sandbox)   │
    │  🖥️  Labs:                                                                   │
    │     • Label namespace for PSS: pod-security.kubernetes.io/enforce=restricted │
    │     • Fix anihpj deployment to pass Restricted PSS (add securityContext)      │
    │     • Configure encryption at rest: EncryptionConfiguration + apiserver flag │
    │     • Verify: create secret, check etcd for encrypted data                    │
    │     • Set up HashiCorp Vault (dev mode) and test Kubernetes auth method       │
    │     • Try gVisor: add runtimeClassName to anihpj pod                          │
    │     • Configure Istio mTLS PeerAuthentication for anihpj namespace            │
    │  ✍️  Exercises:                                                               │
    │     • Compare running anihpj on runc vs gVisor (what do you notice?)          │
    │  📝  Self-Check: Can you write a Restricted PSS-compliant pod YAML?           │
    ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  WEEK 7: SUPPLY CHAIN SECURITY — Trust Nothing, Verify Everything             │
    │  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────                    │
    │  📖 Reading: Chapters 23-27 (Image Scanning, Admission, Static Analysis)      │
    │  🖥️  Labs:                                                                   │
    │     • Run trivy image on anihpj:latest — fix all CRITICAL findings           │
    │     • Set up OPA Gatekeeper: block images from docker.io (allow only private) │
    │     • Set up Kyverno: verifyImages policy (only signed images allowed)        │
    │     • Run kubesec on anihpj deployment YAML (review the score)               │
    │     • Run kube-linter on k8s/ directory (fix all warnings)                   │
    │     • Install cosign, generate keypair, sign anihpj image                     │
    │     • Verify signed image before deployment (Kyverno verifyImages)            │
    │     • Generate SBOM for anihpj: syft anihpj:latest -o spdx-json              │
    │  ✍️  Exercises:                                                               │
    │     • Create a CI/CD pipeline diagram showing all 4 security gates            │
    │  📝  Self-Check: Can you sign an image with cosign and verify it?             │
    ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  WEEK 8: MONITORING & RUNTIME SECURITY — Detect & Respond                     │
    │  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────                    │
    │  📖 Reading: Chapters 28-32 (Audit Logging, Falco, IR, Immutable, Compliance) │
    │  🖥️  Labs:                                                                   │
    │     • Enable audit logging: write audit policy, configure apiserver           │
    │     • Generate audit events: kubectl get secrets, then view audit logs        │
    │     • Install Falco (Helm chart or daemonset)                                │
    │     • Write custom Falco rule: detect shell exec into anihpj pod              │
    │     • Trigger Falco: kubectl exec anihpj -- bash (watch falco alert!)        │
    │     • Simulate incident: malicious pod tries to read /etc/shadow             │
    │     • Configure readOnlyRootFilesystem for anihpj (ensure it still works!)   │
    │     • Run kube-bench as CronJob + send results to Slack/email                │
    │  ✍️  Exercises:                                                               │
    │     • Create incident response runbook: "What do when Falco alerts?"          │
    │  📝  Self-Check: Can you write a Falco rule detecting file access to /etc?    │
    ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  WEEK 9: HANDS-ON — Full anihpj Security Simulation                           │
    │  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────                    │

    Project Structure:
    ─────────────────
    anihpj/                          ← Django Project Root
    ├── manage.py                    ← Django management script
    ├── anihpj/                      ← Project settings package
    │   ├── __init__.py
    │   ├── settings.py              ← Django settings (DB, SECRET_KEY, etc.)
    │   ├── urls.py                  ← Root URL configuration
    │   ├── wsgi.py                  ← WSGI entry point (production)
    │   └── asgi.py                  ← ASGI entry point (async)
    ├── jobpost/                     ← The Job Posting Application
    │   ├── __init__.py
    │   ├── models.py                ← Skills, User, Author, Location, JobPost
    │   ├── views.py                 ← Job listing, filtering, CRUD views
    │   ├── urls.py                  ← App URL routes (/jobs/, /jobs/<id>/, etc.)
    │   ├── admin.py                 ← Django admin registration
    │   ├── apps.py                  ← App configuration
    │   ├── tests.py                 ← Unit/integration tests
    │   └── migrations/              ← Database migration files
    ├── static/                      ← Static files (CSS, JS, images)
    ├── media/                       ← User-uploaded files (resumes, logos)
    ├── templates/                   ← Django HTML templates
    ├── Dockerfile                   ← Container build instructions
    ├── .dockerignore                ← Files excluded from Docker build
    ├── docker-compose.yaml          ← Local dev multi-container setup
    ├── requirements.txt             ← Python dependencies
    └── k8s/                         ← Kubernetes manifests directory
        ├── namespace.yaml
        ├── configmap.yaml
        ├── secret.yaml
        ├── deployment.yaml
        ├── service.yaml
        ├── ingress.yaml
        ├── networkpolicy.yaml
        ├── rbac.yaml
        └── kustomization.yaml

    Data Models (jobpost/models.py):
    ────────────────────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  MODELS IN THE JOBPOST APPLICATION                               │
    │                                                                   │
    │  👤 User (Django Built-in)                                       │
    │     ├── username, email, password (hashed)                        │
    │     ├── first_name, last_name                                    │
    │     └── SECURITY NOTE: email addresses must be encrypted/protected│
    │                                                                   │
    │  🏢 Author                                                       │
    │     ├── name (company/recruiter name)                             │
    │     ├── email (contact email)                                    │
    │     ├── website (optional company URL)                           │
    │     └── SECURITY NOTE: contact info should not be publicly scraped│
    │                                                                   │
    │  📍 Location                                                     │
    │     ├── city, state/province, country                            │
    │     ├── remote (boolean: is remote allowed?)                     │
    │     └── SECURITY NOTE: geo-data privacy considerations           │
    │                                                                   │
    │  🛠️ Skill                                                        │
    │     ├── name (e.g., "Python", "Kubernetes", "React")             │
    │     ├── category (e.g., "Programming", "DevOps", "Design")       │
    │     └── SECURITY NOTE: skill names sanitized to prevent XSS      │
    │                                                                   │
    │  📋 JobPost                                                      │
    │     ├── title (job title)                                        │
    │     ├── description (rich text job description)                  │
    │     ├── author (ForeignKey → Author)                             │
    │     ├── location (ForeignKey → Location)                         │
    │     ├── skills (ManyToManyField → Skill)                         │
    │     ├── salary_min / salary_max (optional range)                 │
    │     ├── posted_date (auto-set on creation)                       │
    │     ├── expiry_date (auto-expire old listings)                   │
    │     ├── is_active (boolean: show/hide listing)                   │
    │     └── SECURITY NOTE: user-submitted content must be sanitized  │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    Generic Dockerfile for anihpj:
    ─────────────────────────────
    [FILE: Dockerfile — Multi-stage build for security and small image size]

    # ============================================
    # STAGE 1: BUILDER — Compile dependencies
    # ============================================
    FROM python:3.11-slim-bookworm AS builder

    # Install system dependencies needed for building Python packages
    RUN apt-get update && apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends \
        build-essential \
        libpq-dev \
        && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*

    # Create a non-root user early (security best practice)
    RUN groupadd -r anihpj && useradd -r -g anihpj -m -s /bin/bash anihpj

    WORKDIR /app

    # Copy only requirements first (leverage Docker layer caching)
    COPY requirements.txt .

    # Install Python dependencies into a virtual environment
    RUN python -m venv /opt/venv && \
        /opt/venv/bin/pip install --no-cache-dir --upgrade pip && \
        /opt/venv/bin/pip install --no-cache-dir -r requirements.txt && \
        /opt/venv/bin/pip install --no-cache-dir gunicorn==21.2.0

    # ============================================
    # STAGE 2: RUNTIME — Minimal production image
    # ============================================
    FROM python:3.11-slim-bookworm

    # Install ONLY runtime dependencies (not build tools)
    RUN apt-get update && apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends \
        libpq5 \
        curl \
        && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/* \
        && apt-get clean

    # Create the same non-root user
    RUN groupadd -r anihpj && useradd -r -g anihpj -m -s /bin/bash anihpj

    # Copy virtual environment from builder stage
    COPY --from=builder /opt/venv /opt/venv

    # Set environment variables
    ENV PATH="/opt/venv/bin:$PATH" \
        PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1 \
        PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 \
        DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE=anihpj.settings

    WORKDIR /app

    # Copy application code (after dependencies for layer caching)
    COPY --chown=anihpj:anihpj . .

    # Create directories for static files and media (writable by non-root)
    RUN mkdir -p /app/staticfiles /app/media && \
        chown -R anihpj:anihpj /app/staticfiles /app/media

    # Switch to non-root user
    USER anihpj

    # Collect static files during build
    RUN python manage.py collectstatic --noinput

    # Expose the port Django/Gunicorn runs on
    EXPOSE 8000

    # Health check
    HEALTHCHECK --interval=30s --timeout=5s --start-period=10s --retries=3 \
        CMD curl -f http://localhost:8000/healthz/ || exit 1

    # Run Gunicorn (production WSGI server)
    CMD ["gunicorn", "anihpj.wsgi:application", \
         "--bind", "0.0.0.0:8000", \
         "--workers", "3", \
         "--threads", "2", \
         "--timeout", "60", \
         "--access-logfile", "-", \
         "--error-logfile", "-", \
         "--max-requests", "1000", \
         "--max-requests-jitter", "100"]

    [END DOCKERFILE]
