Appendix C: Common Security Errors & Solutions
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    [ERROR → CAUSE → SOLUTION TABLE]
    | Error / Symptom                          | Cause                          | Fix                                    |
    | Pod rejected: violates PodSecurity       | PSS label enforces Restricted  | Fix securityContext in pod spec        |
    | Pod fails: AppArmor profile not found    | Profile not loaded on node     | aa-apparmor-parser -r /path/to/profile |
    | NetworkPolicy not blocking traffic       | CNI doesn't support NetPolicy  | Switch to Calico or Cilium             |
    | Falco not detecting events               | Rule condition incorrect       | Use --debug mode, check falco_rules    |
    | etcd data not encrypted                  | EncryptionConfiguration wrong  | Check provider order (aescbc first)    |
    | cosign verify fails                      | Wrong public key used          | Ensure cosign.pub matches cosign.key   |
    | kubectl exec denied                      | RBAC: missing pods/exec verb   | Add pods/exec to Role                  |
    | Seccomp RuntimeDefault breaks app        | App uses unusual syscall       | Use custom seccomp profile with that syscall |
    | Anonymous auth still works after disable | Manifest not updated/applied   | Check kube-apiserver restarted with new flag |
    | SA token still automounts after disable  | Old pod still running          | Delete and let Deployment recreate pod |
    | Audit log not created                    | Log path not writable          | Check /var/log/kubernetes/ permissions |
    | OPA policy not enforcing                 | Constraint not targeting right namespace | Check namespaceSelector in Constraint|
    | ImagePolicyWebhook blocks all images     | Webhook server unreachable     | Check webhook server is running + cert |
    | Trivy: too many false positives          | Old vulnerability DB           | trivy image --update-db               |


Appendix D: Security Terminology Glossary
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    [Alphabetical glossary of 100+ security terms with K8s-specific definitions]

    A
    - Admission Controller: a plugin that intercepts and can modify/reject API requests
    - AppArmor: a Linux security module that restricts process capabilities via profiles
    - Attack Surface: the sum of all possible points where an attacker can try to enter
    - Audit Log: a chronological record of all API server requests
    - Authentication (AuthN): verifying the identity of a user or service ("who are you?")
    - Authorization (AuthZ): determining what an authenticated entity can do ("what can you do?")
    - ABAC: Attribute-Based Access Control — legacy authz mode; avoid, use RBAC instead
    - allowPrivilegeEscalation: securityContext field; false blocks setuid/setgid privilege gain
    - Attestation (in-toto): a signed statement binding an artifact digest to build metadata

    B
    - Base Image: the foundational image layer your Dockerfile builds upon
    - Baseline (PSS): Pod Security Standard level preventing known privilege escalation

    C
    - CA (Certificate Authority): entity that issues and signs digital certificates
    - Capabilities (Linux): fine-grained privileges split from the root superuser role
    - CIS Benchmark: Center for Internet Security configuration security standards
    - ClusterRole: RBAC role granting permissions across all namespaces or cluster-level resources
    - Container Escape: exploiting a vulnerability to break out of a container to the host
    - cosign: a tool for signing and verifying container images (part of Sigstore project)
    - CVE: Common Vulnerabilities and Exposures — a public database of known security flaws
    - CNI: Container Network Interface — plugin standard that implements pod networking and enforces NetworkPolicy (Calico, Cilium)
    - conftest: tool that tests config/YAML against OPA Rego policies
    - CronJob: scheduled workload; a common attacker persistence mechanism to watch for

    D
    - Defense in Depth: using multiple security layers so no single failure is catastrophic
    - distroless: minimal container images containing only the app and its runtime dependencies
    - Dynamic Secret: a secret generated on-demand with a limited lifetime (e.g., Vault DB secrets)

    E
    - eBPF: extended Berkeley Packet Filter — kernel technology used by Falco for event collection
    - Encryption at Rest: encrypting stored data (e.g., Secrets in etcd) so disk reads are safe
    - Encryption in Transit: encrypting data while it moves over the network (TLS)
    - EncryptionConfiguration: API server config file that enables encryption at rest for Secrets in etcd
    - ESO: External Secrets Operator — syncs secrets from external stores (Vault/cloud) into K8s Secrets

    F
    - Falco: open-source runtime security tool that detects anomalous container behaviour
    - 4Cs: Cloud, Cluster, Container, Code — the four layers of cloud native security

    G
    - gVisor: a sandboxed container runtime from Google that intercepts syscalls in userspace
    - GitOps: operational model where Git is the source of truth for infrastructure/app configs

    H
    - HSTS: HTTP Strict Transport Security — tells browsers to always use HTTPS
    - hostNetwork: pod setting that uses the node's network namespace (dangerous)
    - hostPID: pod setting that uses the node's PID namespace (dangerous)

    I
    - Image Digest: a SHA256 hash uniquely identifying an exact image version (immutable)
    - Image Tag: a mutable label on an image (e.g., :latest) — can change to point to new content
    - Immutable Container: a container whose filesystem cannot be modified after start
    - IRSA: IAM Roles for Service Accounts (AWS) — pod-level cloud permission scoping
    - ImagePolicyWebhook: admission webhook that approves or denies images at deploy time
    - IMDS / IMDSv2: cloud Instance Metadata Service (169.254.169.254); v2 requires a session token, resisting SSRF

    J
    - JWT: JSON Web Token — the signed token format used for ServiceAccount authentication

    K
    - Kata Containers: sandboxed container runtime using lightweight VMs per container
    - kube-bench: tool for checking Kubernetes cluster against CIS Benchmark standards
    - Kyverno: Kubernetes-native policy engine using YAML (no Rego needed)
    - kubelet: node agent that runs pods and exposes the node API on port 10250
    - kubesec: tool that scores pod YAML (0-10) for security risk
    - kube-linter: linter for K8s YAML security and production best practices
    - KMS: Key Management Service — external key provider for envelope-encrypting Secrets at rest

    L
    - Lateral Movement: after breaching one system, moving to others within the same network
    - Least Privilege: principle of granting only the minimum permissions required

    M
    - mTLS: mutual TLS — both client and server verify each other's identity
    - Multi-stage Build: Dockerfile pattern to compile in one stage, run in a minimal final stage

    N
    - NetworkPolicy: Kubernetes resource defining allowed pod-to-pod network traffic rules
    - NodeRestriction: admission plugin limiting what each kubelet can modify via the API
    - Non-root Container: container running as a non-root Linux user (UID > 0)

    O
    - OIDC: OpenID Connect — identity protocol used for Kubernetes authentication
    - OPA: Open Policy Agent — general-purpose policy engine using Rego language
    - OPA Gatekeeper: Kubernetes-specific integration of OPA via admission webhooks

    P
    - Pod Security Admission: the built-in K8s admission controller enforcing PSS levels
    - Pod Security Standards (PSS): built-in K8s policy profiles (Privileged/Baseline/Restricted)
    - Privileged Container: container with full host root access (extremely dangerous)
    - Privilege Escalation: gaining more permissions than originally granted
    - Provenance: a verifiable record of where and how an artifact was built (see SLSA)
    - Projected Volume: volume type that delivers bound ServiceAccount tokens with audience and expiry

    Q
    - Quota (ResourceQuota): per-namespace cap on resource consumption and object counts (anti-DoS, tenant isolation)

    R
    - RBAC: Role-Based Access Control — the primary K8s authorization system
    - readOnlyRootFilesystem: securityContext field making the container filesystem immutable
    - Rego: OPA's policy language (functional, declarative)
    - Restricted (PSS): most secure Pod Security Standard level
    - Rekor: Sigstore's tamper-evident transparency log recording signatures
    - RuntimeClass: pod field selecting the container runtime (e.g., gVisor, Kata) for sandboxing
    - RuntimeDefault (seccomp): the container runtime's default syscall-filter profile

    S
    - SBOM: Software Bill of Materials — list of all components in a software artifact
    - Seccomp: Secure Computing Mode — kernel feature filtering which syscalls a process can make
    - SecurityContext: pod/container field defining security settings (user, caps, seccomp, etc.)
    - ServiceAccount: Kubernetes identity assigned to pods for API server access
    - Sigstore: ecosystem of tools for supply chain security (cosign, fulcio, rekor)
    - SLSA: Supply-chain Levels for Software Artifacts — framework for build provenance
    - Static Pod: pod defined by a YAML file on a node and managed directly by kubelet
    - SSRF: Server-Side Request Forgery — tricking a server into making requests (e.g., to the metadata API)
    - syft: tool that generates SBOMs from container images (SPDX/CycloneDX)
    - SPIFFE: standard for cryptographic workload identity, used by service meshes for mTLS

    T
    - TLS: Transport Layer Security — cryptographic protocol for securing network connections
    - TokenReview: API to validate a ServiceAccount JWT token
    - Trivy: open-source vulnerability scanner for container images, filesystems, and repos
    - Threat Model: structured analysis of what can go wrong and how (e.g., STRIDE)
    - Trivy Operator: in-cluster controller that continuously scans running images for new CVEs

    U
    - UID (runAsUser): the Linux user ID a container runs as; non-zero means non-root
    - User Namespace: remaps container UIDs to unprivileged host UIDs for stronger isolation

    V
    - Vault (HashiCorp): enterprise secret management with dynamic secrets and audit logs
    - ValidatingWebhookConfiguration: K8s resource defining an external policy webhook
    - verifyImages (Kyverno): policy rule that enforces valid cosign signatures before admission

    W
    - WireGuard: fast in-kernel VPN; Cilium uses it for transparent pod-to-pod encryption
    - Webhook (Admission): external HTTP service that validates or mutates API requests at admission

    X
    - X.509: the digital certificate standard used for TLS and Kubernetes client/component certificates

    Y
    - YAML: the declarative, indentation-sensitive config format for all Kubernetes manifests

    Z
    - Zero Trust: security model assuming no implicit trust, verify every request

Appendix F: CKS Exam Preparation Checklist & Resources
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    F.1  Pre-Exam Preparation Checklist
         [CHECKLIST: 30-item checklist covering knowledge, tools, and logistics]

         Knowledge checklist:
         □  Can write NetworkPolicy from memory (default deny + specific allow)
         □  Know all 6 PSS policy fields in Restricted level
         □  Can write full security context (non-root, caps, seccomp, AppArmor)
         □  Know audit policy levels: None, Metadata, Request, RequestResponse
         □  Can write Falco rule from scratch (condition + output + priority)
         □  Know file paths: kube-apiserver.yaml, kubelet config, falco rules
         □  Can configure encryption at rest (EncryptionConfiguration YAML)
         □  Know how to check AppArmor status and load profiles
         □  Know kube-bench: how to run and read output
         □  Can configure RBAC: Role/ClusterRole + Binding from memory
         □  Know OPA Gatekeeper: ConstraintTemplate structure
         □  Know Kyverno: validate/mutate/verifyImages policy structure
         □  Can run Trivy and interpret severity output
         □  Know cosign: sign and verify workflow
         □  Can write mTLS PeerAuthentication (Istio) YAML

         Exam day logistics:
         □  Bookmark: kubernetes.io/docs, falco.org/docs, sigstore.dev
         □  Test your webcam, microphone, internet connection
         □  Prepare quiet room, desk clear of papers
         □  Know your PSI Secure Browser installation works
         □  Have government photo ID ready

    F.2  Recommended Practice Platforms

Appendix G: 10-Week CKS Learning Path
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    G.1  Week-by-Week Study Calendar
         - A 10-week roadmap from CKA-certified to CKS-ready (~7-11 hrs/week)
         - Each week: chapters to read + hands-on labs + a verification checkpoint
         [CALENDAR: 10-week visual calendar — chapters, labs, mock exams per week]

         [10-WEEK CKS PATH — chapters → focus per week]
         ┌──────┬────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ WEEK │ CHAPTERS       │ FOCUS / MILESTONE                            │
         ├──────┼────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────┤
         │ 1    │ Ch 1-3         │ Foundation, threat model, 4Cs, architecture │
         │ 2    │ Ch 4-7         │ Cluster Setup: CIS, NetPol, Ingress TLS     │
         │ 3    │ Ch 8-12        │ Cluster Hardening: API, RBAC, SA, upgrades   │
         │ 4    │ Ch 13-16       │ System Hardening: OS, AppArmor, Seccomp     │
         │ 5    │ Ch 17-19       │ PSS, SecurityContext, Secrets encryption    │
         │ 6    │ Ch 20-22       │ Vault, sandboxing, mTLS / Pod encryption    │
         │ 7    │ Ch 23-25       │ Supply chain: images, admission, static scan│
         │ 8    │ Ch 26-27       │ Signing (cosign), SBOM, SLSA, provenance    │
         │ 9    │ Ch 28-32       │ Audit, Falco, incident response, compliance │
         │ 10   │ Ch 33-38 +mock │ Hands-on project, mocks, last-mile review   │
         └──────┴────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    G.2  Weekly Study Routine & Progress Tracking
         - Per week: read → lab → self-test with that domain's practice questions
         - Checkpoint: don't advance until you can do the week's labs from memory

Appendix H: Hands-On Lab Workbook
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    H.1  How to Use This Workbook
         - 56 timed labs across the 6 CKS domains + a 17-task, 120-minute mock
         - Each lab: objective + context (cluster/namespace) + time box + solution
         - The gap between reading and passing at 95%+ is DOING these under time
         [SCORING RUBRIC: 100% works+verified / 75% works / 50% partial / 0% none]

    H.2  Domain 1 — Cluster Setup Labs (10 labs, CKS 15%)
         [LABS: CIS/kube-bench fixes, NetworkPolicy, Ingress TLS, node metadata]

    H.3  Domain 2 — Cluster Hardening Labs (10 labs, CKS 15%)
         [LABS: RBAC least-privilege, ServiceAccount lockdown, API restriction, upgrade]

    H.4  Domain 3 — System Hardening Labs (8 labs, CKS 10%)
         [LABS: OS minimization, AppArmor profile, Seccomp profile, IAM/binary verify]

    H.5  Domain 4 — Minimize Microservice Vulnerabilities Labs (10 labs, CKS 20%)
         [LABS: PSS, SecurityContext, Secrets encryption, Vault, sandboxing, mTLS]

    H.6  Domain 5 — Supply Chain Security Labs (10 labs, CKS 20%)
         [LABS: Trivy scan, permitted registries, cosign sign/verify, SBOM, static analysis]

    H.7  Domain 6 — Monitoring, Logging & Runtime Security Labs (8 labs, CKS 20%)
         [LABS: audit policy, Falco rules, incident response, immutability, compliance]

    H.8  Timed Mock Exam — 17 Tasks in 120 Minutes
         - Full simulated exam: 17 tasks weighted across all 6 domains
         - Strict rules: 2-hour clock, docs only, verify every task
         [MOCK: 17 tasks with context, time box, and points; solutions in a fold-out]
